=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
All KEYENCE product names consist of two uppercase English letters.
They have already used N product names, the i-th of which (1\leq i\leq N) is S_i.
Once a product name is used, it cannot be reused, so they decided to create an NG (Not Good) list to quickly identify previously used product names.
The NG list must satisfy the following conditions.

- It consists of one or more strings, each consisting of uppercase English letters.
- For each already used product name, there exists at least one string in the list that contains the name as a (contiguous) substring.
- None of the strings in the list contain any length-2 (contiguous) substring that is not an already used product name.

Find the minimum possible number of strings in the NG list.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
S_1
S_2
\vdots
S_N

Output

Print the minimum possible number of strings in the NG list.

Constraints


- 1\leq N\leq 26^2
- N is an integer.
- Each S_i is a string of length 2 consisting of uppercase English letters.
- All S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_N are distinct.

Sample Input 1

7
AB
BC
CA
CD
DE
DF
XX

Sample Output 1

3

One NG list satisfying the conditions is the one consisting of the following three strings:

- CABCDE
- DF
- XX

This has three strings, and there is no NG list satisfying the conditions with 2 or fewer strings, so print 3.

Sample Input 2

5
AC
BC
CD
DE
DF

Sample Output 2

2

One NG list satisfying the conditions is the one consisting of the following two strings:

- ACDE
- BCDF

Note that each used product name may appear in multiple strings in the NG list or multiple times within the same string.

Sample Input 3

6
AB
AC
CB
AD
DB
BA

Sample Output 3

1

For example, an NG list consisting only of ABACBADB satisfies the conditions.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, let's revisit the problem statement: "All KEYENCE product names consist of two uppercase English letters. They have already used N product names... NG list must satisfy: It consists of one or more strings, each consisting of uppercase English letters. For each already used product name, there exists at least one string in the list that contains the name as a (contiguous) substring. None of the strings in the list contain any length-2 (contiguous) substring that is not an already used product name."

Important nuance: "None of the strings in the list contain any length-2 substring that is not an already used product name." This means every length-2 substring in every string must be one of the used product names. Now, let's think about the max flow lower bound more rigorously. Suppose we have final H with P_final = K. Let A be added duplicates. We want show K >= P0 - F. Consider the multiset A. Decompose A into paths and cycles. Let R be the number of paths in a decomposition that start at a vertex with original deficit and end at a vertex with original surplus. Each such path corresponds to a unit of flow in the reachability network, so R <= F. The change in P from adding A is at most R? Need show P_final >= P0 - R (or P0 - F). Is it true that each path not of this type does not reduce P, and each of this type reduces by at most 1, so total reduction <= R? Let's verify with a path from original deficit p to original surplus q: P decreases by exactly 1 if p remains <=0 and q remains >=0. But if p has deficit magnitude d and we add multiple paths, after d paths p=0; further paths starting at p would make p positive, increasing P. In a decomposition of A, paths are just units; if we send more than d from p, the extra would start at p after it's no longer deficit, and would not reduce P (could increase). But max flow F respects capacities -b_p and b_q, so it won't send more than deficit/surplus. However arbitrary A could send more; then reduction less. So reduction <= number of matched units within capacities <= F. Thus K >= P0 - F. Achievability: choose a max flow of value F; for each unit of flow from p to q, add a directed path p->q. This adds F units of deficit-to-surplus paths, reducing P by F. Need ensure after adding all paths, no vertex's b crosses from negative to positive or positive to negative in a way that reduces less? Since flow respects capacities, total added paths starting at p <= -b_p, so b_p increases by at most -b_p, so b_p <=0. Total added paths ending at q <= b_q, so b_q decreases by at most b_q, so b_q >=0. Thus each unit reduces P by exactly 1. Internal vertices balanced. So P_final = P0 - F. Good. If F > P0? no.

def find(x): while parent[x]!=x: parent[x]=parent[parent[x]]; x=parent[x]; return x

Dinic class:
class Dinic:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n
        self.ad=[[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        self.ad[fr].append([to, cap, len(self.ad[to])])
        self.ad[to].append([fr, 0, len(self.ad[fr])-1])
But using list references can be tricky. Better use Edge class with to, rev, cap. Or use arrays.

Since Python code final only, implement simple.

```
class Edge:
    __slots__ = ('to','rev','cap')
    def __init__(self,to,rev,cap):
        self.to=to; self.rev=rev; self.cap=cap
```

add_edge:
```
self.g[fr].append(Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap))
self.g[to].append(Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr])-1, 0))
```

BFS levels:
```
from collections import deque
def bfs(s,t):
    level=[-1]*n
    q=deque([s]); level[s]=0
    while q:
       v=q.popleft()
       for e in g[v]:
          if e.cap>0 and level[e.to]<0:
             level[e.to]=level[v]+1
             q.append(e.to)
    self.level=level
    return level[t]>=0
```

DFS iterative recursion:
```
def dfs(v,t,f):
    if v==t: return f
    for i in range(it[v], len(g[v])):
       e=g[v][i]
       if e.cap>0 and level[v]<level[e.to]:
          ret=dfs(e.to,t,min(f,e.cap))
          if ret:
             e.cap-=ret
             g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
             return ret
       it[v]+=1
    return 0
```
Need update iterator correctly. Common:
```
while it[v] < len(g[v]):
    e = g[v][it[v]]
    if e.cap > 0 and level[v] < level[e.to]:
       d = dfs(...)
       if d:
          ...
          return d
    it[v] += 1
return 0
```
Max flow:
```
flow=0
INF=10**9
while bfs:
   it=[0]*n
   while True:
      f=dfs(s,t,INF)
      if f==0: break
      flow+=f
```

Implementation of Floyd:
```
reach = [[False]*26 for _ in range(26)]
for i in range(26):
    reach[i][i] = True
for u,v in edges:
    reach[u][v] = True
for k in range(26):
    for i in range(26):
        if reach[i][k]:
            for j in range(26):
                if reach[k][j]:
                    reach[i][j] = True
```
This is O(26^3) ~ 17576, fine. Note: If reach[i][k] true and reach[k][j] true, set. Since reach[k][k] true, okay.

Let's try to prove the formula in a concise way for ourselves:
- For a fixed multigraph H (copies of used edges), minimum strings = sum over weak components max(1, P(H)).
- We can increase multiplicities. Let original P0. Adding a copy of a directed path from a deficit vertex to a surplus vertex decreases P by 1. Adding any other edges does not decrease P. Therefore max decrease is max flow from deficits to surpluses in reachability graph. So min P = P0 - F, and min strings per component = max(1, P0-F).
This is the core.

Now, let's consider if the DSU union should be done before or after reading? We can union as we read. Since edges distinct, no issue. But if we union as we read, root changes; has_edge later.

Implementation outline:

```
import sys
from collections import deque

class Dinic:
    ...
def solve():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N=int(input())
    parent=list(range(26))
    def find(x):
        while parent[x]!=x:
            parent[x]=parent[parent[x]]
            x=parent[x]
        return x
    def union(a,b):
        ra=find(a); rb=find(b)
        if ra!=rb:
            parent[rb]=ra
    out=[0]*26; inn=[0]*26
    edges=[]
    for _ in range(N):
        s=input().strip()
        u=ord(s[0])-65; v=ord(s[1])-65
        edges.append((u,v))
        out[u]+=1; inn[v]+=1
        union(u,v)
    # reach
    reach=[[False]*26 for _ in range(26)]
    for i in range(26):
        reach[i][i]=True
    for u,v in edges:
        reach[u][v]=True
    for k in range(26):
        for i in range(26):
            if reach[i][k]:
                for j in range(26):
                    if reach[k][j]:
                        reach[i][j]=True
    b=[out[i]-inn[i] for i in range(26)]
    # group components
    comps={}
    for i in range(26):
        r=find(i)
        comps.setdefault(r,[]).append(i)
    ans=0
    for verts in comps.values():
        if all(out[v]+inn[v]==0 for v in verts):
            continue
        P0=sum(max(0,b[v]) for v in verts)
        if P0==0:
            ans+=1
            continue
        # Dinic
        S=26; T=27
        dinic=Dinic(28)
        for v in verts:
            if b[v]<0:
                dinic.add_edge(S, v, -b[v])
            elif b[v]>0:
                dinic.add_edge(v, T, b[v])
        for v in verts:
            if b[v]<0:
                for u in verts:
                    if b[u]>0 and reach[v][u]:
                        dinic.add_edge(v, u, 10**9)
        flow=dinic.max_flow(S,T)
        ans += max(1, P0 - flow)
    print(ans)
```

Need ensure `find` is defined before union calls. Yes.

Potential issue: In DSU union, if we union as we read, parent[rb]=ra without rank; find with path compression okay for 26. But if union self-loop, ra==rb no change.

Potential issue: `comps` uses roots after all unions. Since parent may not be fully compressed, find(i) returns root. Good.

Potential issue: If a component has edges but all out+in==0? impossible. For self-loop out+in=2. For edge AB, out[A]+inn[A]=1, out[B]+inn[B]=1. Good.

Potential issue: If a component has P0=0 but has edges, ans+=1. If a component has P0>0, flow computed. If flow maybe >P0? No, source caps sum P0. But due to INF edges, max flow <= source cap sum = P0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a component has P0>0 but no deficits? impossible. If no deficits, P0=0.

Let's write Dinic carefully:

```
class Edge:
    __slots__ = ('to','rev','cap')
    def __init__(self,to,rev,cap):
        self.to=to
        self.rev=rev
        self.cap=cap

class Dinic:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n
        self.g=[[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        fwd=Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
        rev=Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
        self.g[fr].append(fwd)
        self.g[to].append(rev)
    def bfs(self,s,t):
        self.level=[-1]*self.n
        q=deque([s])
        self.level[s]=0
        while q:
            v=q.popleft()
            for e in self.g[v]:
                if e.cap>0 and self.level[e.to]<0:
                    self.level[e.to]=self.level[v]+1
                    if e.to==t:
                        # can return? We can continue or return; returning early okay but level of other nodes not needed? Dinic BFS can return early? It may still work but safer continue.
                    q.append(e.to)
        return self.level[t]>=0
    def dfs(self,v,t,f):
        if v==t:
            return f
        for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.g[v])):
            self.it[v]=i
            e=self.g[v][i]
            if e.cap>0 and self.level[v]<self.level[e.to]:
                ret=self.dfs(e.to,t,min(f,e.cap))
                if ret:
                    e.cap-=ret
                    self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                    return ret
        self.it[v]=len(self.g[v])
        return 0
```
Need be careful with iterator update. Common pattern:

```
def dfs(self, v, t, f):
    if v == t:
        return f
    for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.g[v])):
        self.it[v] = i
        e = self.g[v][i]
        if e.cap > 0 and self.level[v] < self.level[e.to]:
            ret = self.dfs(e.to, t, min(f, e.cap))
            if ret:
                e.cap -= ret
                self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                return ret
    self.it[v] = len(self.g[v])
    return 0
```
But if we set `self.it[v] = i` before recursive call, and the edge fails (ret=0), the loop will increment i? Actually `for i in range(self.it[v], len(...))` uses current it[v] as start. If we set it[v]=i and then ret=0, next iteration i+1, but it[v] remains i until set again at next iteration. At next iteration, `range(self.it[v], len)` starts at i again, causing infinite loop? Let's implement standard:

```
def dfs(self, v, t, f):
    if v == t:
        return f
    while self.it[v] < len(self.g[v]):
        e = self.g[v][self.it[v]]
        if e.cap > 0 and self.level[v] < self.level[e.to]:
            ret = self.dfs(e.to, t, min(f, e.cap))
            if ret:
                e.cap -= ret
                self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                return ret
        self.it[v] += 1
    return 0
```
This is standard. Use recursion.

BFS:
```
def bfs(self,s,t):
    self.level=[-1]*self.n
    q=deque([s])
    self.level[s]=0
    while q:
        v=q.popleft()
        for e in self.g[v]:
            if e.cap>0 and self.level[e.to]<0:
                self.level[e.to]=self.level[v]+1
                q.append(e.to)
    return self.level[t]>=0
```

Max_flow:
```
def max_flow(self,s,t):
    flow=0
    INF=10**18
    while self.bfs(s,t):
        self.it=[0]*self.n
        while True:
            f=self.dfs(s,t,INF)
            if f==0:
                break
            flow+=f
    return flow
```

Since capacities small, INF=10**9 enough.

Now, let's think about if there is a bug in Edge rev indices:
```
fwd = Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
rev = Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
self.g[fr].append(fwd)
self.g[to].append(rev)
```
For fwd.rev should be index of rev in g[to] before appending rev, which is len(g[to]) before append. We set fwd.rev = len(g[to]) (old). Then append fwd to g[fr]. Then rev.rev should be index of fwd in g[fr], which is len(g[fr]) before appending fwd? Actually after appending fwd, its index is len(g[fr]) old. We set rev.rev = len(self.g[fr]) before appending fwd? In code above, we create rev with rev=len(self.g[fr]) before append fwd? Let's do:
```
self.g[fr].append(Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap))
self.g[to].append(Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr])-1, 0))
```
Because after appending fwd, its index is len(g[fr])-1. This is common. Let's use this.

```
def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
    fwd = Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
    rev = Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
    self.g[fr].append(fwd)
    self.g[to].append(rev)
```
Wait if we create rev with rev=len(self.g[fr]) before appending fwd, then fwd will be at index len(self.g[fr]) old, which equals rev.rev. Good. fwd.rev = len(self.g[to]) old, which will be index of rev after appending rev to g[to]. This works:
- Before append: len(g[fr]) = Lfr, len(g[to]) = Lto.
- fwd.rev = Lto.
- rev.rev = Lfr.
- Append fwd to g[fr] at index Lfr.
- Append rev to g[to] at index Lto.
So fwd.rev points to rev (index Lto), rev.rev points to fwd (index Lfr). Good.
So code:
```
fwd = Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
rev = Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
self.g[fr].append(fwd)
self.g[to].append(rev)
```
Yes.

Now, let's consider if the original graph can be decomposed into P0 trails in a way that the endpoints (deficits/surpluses) are paired differently, affecting which connectors are needed. The max flow over all deficits/surpluses accounts for choosing a decomposition? Wait, the trail decomposition of original H (m=1) is not unique. The starts and ends at each vertex are determined by b: number of trails starting at v minus ending at v = b(v). But the actual number of trails starting at a surplus vertex can be more than b(v), because some trails may also end there. However the total number of trails is P0, and at each surplus vertex with b>0, there are at least b(v) more starts than ends. The exact starts/ends can vary. But is it always possible to choose a trail decomposition such that the starts/ends correspond to the matched pairs? The standard decomposition with P0 trails can be constructed by adding dummy edges from deficits to surpluses and then removing them. The dummy edges define which deficit connects to which surplus in the Eulerian circuit. We can choose any pairing of deficits to surpluses for dummy edges? To balance, we need add P0 dummy edges from deficit vertices to surplus vertices. We can choose any multiset of dummy edges with supplies deficits and demands surpluses. If we choose dummy edges according to the max flow matching? Wait dummy edges are not original edges; they are artificial. In the proof, to decompose H into P0 trails, we add P0 dummy edges from deficit vertices to surplus vertices (any pairing) to make balanced, find Eulerian circuit, then remove dummy edges. The resulting trails start at surplus and end at deficit according to dummy edges. So we can choose the pairing of deficits to surpluses arbitrarily for the original decomposition. Then to merge trails, we need a path from the deficit of one trail to the surplus of another. The max flow matching of deficits to surpluses via reachability tells how many such connections are possible. But can we simultaneously choose the original trail decomposition to align with the flow? I think yes, because the flow paths themselves are original edges; we can add them as duplicate edges to H, then decompose the resulting H. We don't need to first decompose original and then merge; we directly construct H with duplicates and decompose into max(1,P0-F) trails. The max flow paths are added, and the final degree conditions guarantee a decomposition. So no issue.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from collections import deque


class Edge:
    __slots__ = ("to", "rev", "cap")

    def __init__(self, to, rev, cap):
        self.to = to
        self.rev = rev
        self.cap = cap


class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.g = [[] for _ in range(n)]

    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        fwd = Edge(to, len(self.g[to]), cap)
        rev = Edge(fr, len(self.g[fr]), 0)
        self.g[fr].append(fwd)
        self.g[to].append(rev)

    def bfs(self, s, t):
        self.level = [-1] * self.n
        q = deque([s])
        self.level[s] = 0
        while q:
            v = q.popleft()
            for e in self.g[v]:
                if e.cap > 0 and self.level[e.to] < 0:
                    self.level[e.to] = self.level[v] + 1
                    q.append(e.to)
        return self.level[t] >= 0

    def dfs(self, v, t, f):
        if v == t:
            return f
        while self.it[v] < len(self.g[v]):
            e = self.g[v][self.it[v]]
            if e.cap > 0 and self.level[e.to] == self.level[v] + 1:
                ret = self.dfs(e.to, t, min(f, e.cap))
                if ret:
                    e.cap -= ret
                    self.g[e.to][e.rev].cap += ret
                    return ret
            self.it[v] += 1
        return 0

    def max_flow(self, s, t):
        flow = 0
        INF = 10**18
        while self.bfs(s, t):
            self.it = [0] * self.n
            while True:
                f = self.dfs(s, t, INF)
                if f == 0:
                    break
                flow += f
        return flow


def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    N = int(data[0])
    edges = []
    out = [0] * 26
    inn = [0] * 26

    parent = list(range(26))

    def find(x):
        while parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
            x = parent[x]
        return x

    def union(a, b):
        ra = find(a)
        rb = find(b)
        if ra != rb:
            parent[rb] = ra

    for i in range(N):
        s = data[i + 1]
        u = ord(s[0]) - ord('A')
        v = ord(s[1]) - ord('A')
        edges.append((u, v))
        out[u] += 1
        inn[v] += 1
        union(u, v)

    # reachability in the directed graph
    reach = [[False] * 26 for _ in range(26)]
    for i in range(26):
        reach[i][i] = True
    for u, v in edges:
        reach[u][v] = True

    for k in range(26):
        for i in range(26):
            if reach[i][k]:
                for j in range(26):
                    if reach[k][j]:
                        reach[i][j] = True

    bal = [out[i] - inn[i] for i in range(26)]

    comps = {}
    for i in range(26):
        r = find(i)
        comps.setdefault(r, []).append(i)

    ans = 0
    INF = 10**9

    for verts in comps.values():
        if all(out[v] + inn[v] == 0 for v in verts):
            continue

        p0 = sum(max(0, bal[v]) for v in verts)

        if p0 == 0:
            ans += 1
            continue

        S = 26
        T = 27
        dinic = Dinic(28)

        for v in verts:
            if bal[v] < 0:
                dinic.add_edge(S, v, -bal[v])
            elif bal[v] > 0:
                dinic.add_edge(v, T, bal[v])

        for v in verts:
            if bal[v] < 0:
                for u in verts:
                    if bal[u] > 0 and reach[v][u]:
                        dinic.add_edge(v, u, INF)

        flow = dinic.max_flow(S, T)
        ans += max(1, p0 - flow)

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
